##
malwaretech@infosec.exchange at 2026-11-03T06:44:52.000Z ##

Update on the 9.8 CVSS TCP/IP RCE in the Linux kernel (CVE-2026-98432): we've detected what appears to be a threat actor activity scanning the entire internet for exposed affected systems. Our own scanning also suggests that the number of potential targets is high, therefore there is an increased likelihood of threat actors attempting to develop exploits for the vulnerability.

while CVE-2026-98432 is believed to require MPTCP to be enabled in the kernel, as well as a listening app/service which binds an MPTCP socket, which many have suggested should be rare, several kernels including Debian enable MPTCP by default.

Additionally, Go 1.24+ binds MPTCP sockets by default if the option is enabled in the kernel, so anything built on top of Go (including Caddy) will create the required MPTCP listener.

The mitigation of setting net.mptcp.enabled=0 disabled MPTCP, but does not apply to existing sockets, so any applications that have bound MPTCP sockets need to be restarted, otherwise the system remains exploitable.

You can check if your server is running any MPTCP listeners with the following command: sudo ss -Mlnp

On 2026-10-30 our preliminary internet-wide scan of port 443 confirmed that there are approximately 2,102,043 servers that are successfully reachable via an MPTCP connection. A deeper scan which attempts to connect to each endpoint on port 443 and negotiate a full TLS connection with MPTCP enabled returned about 762,774 systems.

Neither scan can confirm that these are unpatched kernels, just total exposure, but it's a lot more systems than expected. Since a valid HTTP connection is not required to exploit the vulnerability, the total attack surface is likely closer to the 2.1 million number than the 763k one.

As of today (2026-11-02), we've picked up several residential proxies and VPNs scanning the internet on port 443 using MPTCP-enabled connections. Since regular scanners do not negotiate MPTCP by default, and no data is being sent to the HTTP server over 443, we strongly suspect this activity is scanning for CVE-2026-98432 specifically. The use of residential proxies and RU-based VPN servers suggests that this is likely threat actor scanning, rather than security researchers.

Currently there are no known publicly available remote code execution exploits, but there are several denial of service proof-of-concepts floating around. This code gives threat actors a starting point to begin attempting to weaponize the vulnerability. Building exploits for the vulnerability is reported to be extremely difficult, but several state-sponsored threat actors likely have the resources required to succeed.

##
DarkWebInformer@infosec.exchange at 2026-11-02T16:27:19.000Z ##

🚨 Critical Linux kernel flaw could allow remote root code execution; CVE-2026-98432

A use-after-free vulnerability in the Linux IPv4 TCP handshake path allows an unauthenticated attacker to trigger memory corruption by sending specially timed packets to a listening service.

Successful exploitation could crash the system or potentially execute arbitrary code as root.
⠀
• CVSS: 9.8
• Affected: Linux kernel 4.4 and later
• Attack vector: Network
• Privileges required: None
• User interaction: None
• Active exploitation: Not reported
⠀
Fixed kernel branches:

• 5.10.272
• 5.15.223
• 6.1.190
• 6.6.159
• 6.12.113
• 6.18.56
• 7.2.10
• 7.3
⠀
Administrators should install their distribution’s updated kernel and reboot to activate the fix.

Advisory: https://access.redhat.com/security/vulnerabilities/RHSB-2026-016

##
nixCraft@mastodon.social at 2026-11-02T15:28:37.000Z ##

WTF Ubuntu? https://chalmovsky.com/posts/wtf-ubuntu

In case you missed it: CVE-2026-98432 is a critical (CVSS 9.8) bug in the Linux kernel's TCP code. It's been there since 2016, so virtually every Linux server on the planet has it, and it can be hit remotely. Anthropic says it built a working exploit that gets root on a server. A total takeover.

Debian patched it on October 16. Ubuntu still doesn't have a patch more than two weeks later. Every supported release, LTS included, is listed as "Vulnerable".

##
technotenshi@infosec.exchange at 2026-11-01T06:45:08.000Z ##

Linux kernel CVE-2026-98432 is a use-after-free in the IPv4 TCP handshake that a remote, unauthenticated peer can reach. Upstream is fixed, but Ubuntu's tracker still lists its kernels as vulnerable and Red Hat lists RHEL 8, 9 and 10 as affected. Anthropic says Claude built a root exploit for servers with a multipath TCP listener, which Go 1.24+ enables by default. The Go team says the bug is not specific to MPTCP.

https://atlasum.net/blog/cve-2026-98432-linux-ipv4-tcp-uaf

#LinuxKernel #InfoSec

##
kmankarjr19@infosec.exchange at 2026-11-02T20:30:02.000Z ##

3 threats SOC teams should know about this week 🛡️

My site Intel60 tracked 119 security stories this week. 34 were rated critical, and CISA added 10 bugs to its Known Exploited Vulnerabilities list. These are the three I’d put at the top of a SOC’s list:

1️⃣ Cisco firewall VPN zero-day (CVE-2026-76418)
Attackers are exploiting a flaw in the remote-access VPN of Cisco Secure Firewall ASA and FTD. It’s the second Cisco firewall zero-day since August.
🔎 SOC angle: patch now, then review VPN logs for unusual sessions. Edge devices are where attackers get in first.

2️⃣ Claude Code extension backdoored in Open VSX
The extension in the Open VSX registry was compromised to install a persistent SSH backdoor on developer machines. Cursor’s marketplace was also affected and temporarily shut down.
🔎 SOC angle: check developer endpoints for unexpected SSH keys and new authorized_keys entries. Developer tools are now part of the supply-chain attack surface.

3️⃣ Ivanti Connect Secure flaws added to KEV
CISA flagged a missing-authentication flaw (CVE-2026-111357) that can be chained with other bugs. The federal deadline is Nov 2, which tells you how urgent CISA considers it.
🔎 SOC angle: if you run Ivanti, patch, then hunt for signs of compromise. With Ivanti, patching alone hasn’t always removed attackers who were already in.

The pattern this week: the entry points are edge devices and developer tools. Watch what sits at the perimeter and what your developers install.

I track these with Intel60, a free tool I built that turns security news into 60-word, severity-rated briefs every 30 minutes: https://intel60.app

What was the biggest story on your radar this week? 👇

#cybersecurity #SOC #threatintel #infosec #blueteam #vulnerabilitymanagement

##
DarkWebInformer@infosec.exchange at 2026-11-01T17:19:45.000Z ##

🚨 Critical LXD and MicroCloud authentication bypass rated CVSS 10.0

CVE-2026-112816 allows an unauthenticated attacker to impersonate a trusted TLS client using only its public certificate, potentially gaining full access to clusters, virtual machines and the host as root.
⠀
The attacker must be able to reach the HTTPS API and obtain the public certificate of an existing trusted client.
⠀
• CVSS: 10.0
• Affected: LXD 4.0.14, 5.0.10, 5.21.8 and 6.9
• Fixed: LXD 4.0.15, 5.0.11, 5.21.9 and 6.10.1
• MicroCloud fixes: 2.1.5 and 3.4.1
• Active exploitation: Not reported
• Public exploit: None reported
⠀
Administrators should update immediately and restrict access to the HTTPS API.

A vulnerability allowing an unauthenticated attacker to perform remote code execution (RCE) on servers running the Veeam Virtualization Extensions service of Veeam Management Pack for Microsoft System Center.

beyondmachines1 at 2026-11-03T11:01:02.179Z ##

Veeam Patches Critical Remote Code Execution Flaws in Management Pack for MS System Center

Veeam has patched three vulnerabilities in its Management Pack for Microsoft System Center, including a critical unauthenticated remote code execution flaw (CVE-2023-22793). These bugs allow attackers to take control of monitoring servers and potentially move laterally through virtualization environments.

**If you use Veeam Management Pack for Microsoft System Center version 9.x, upgrade to version 13.1.0.334 ASAP. These flaws let attackers take full control of your monitoring server. If you're on a build older than 9a (9.1.0.3107), update to 9a first, then upgrade to 13.1.0.334, since Veeam won't release a separate fix for 9.x.**
#cybersecurity #infosec #advisory #vulnerability

kmankarjr19@infosec.exchange at 2026-11-02T20:30:02.000Z ##

3 threats SOC teams should know about this week 🛡️

My site Intel60 tracked 119 security stories this week. 34 were rated critical, and CISA added 10 bugs to its Known Exploited Vulnerabilities list. These are the three I’d put at the top of a SOC’s list:

1️⃣ Cisco firewall VPN zero-day (CVE-2026-76418)
Attackers are exploiting a flaw in the remote-access VPN of Cisco Secure Firewall ASA and FTD. It’s the second Cisco firewall zero-day since August.
🔎 SOC angle: patch now, then review VPN logs for unusual sessions. Edge devices are where attackers get in first.

2️⃣ Claude Code extension backdoored in Open VSX
The extension in the Open VSX registry was compromised to install a persistent SSH backdoor on developer machines. Cursor’s marketplace was also affected and temporarily shut down.
🔎 SOC angle: check developer endpoints for unexpected SSH keys and new authorized_keys entries. Developer tools are now part of the supply-chain attack surface.

3️⃣ Ivanti Connect Secure flaws added to KEV
CISA flagged a missing-authentication flaw (CVE-2026-111357) that can be chained with other bugs. The federal deadline is Nov 2, which tells you how urgent CISA considers it.
🔎 SOC angle: if you run Ivanti, patch, then hunt for signs of compromise. With Ivanti, patching alone hasn’t always removed attackers who were already in.

The pattern this week: the entry points are edge devices and developer tools. Watch what sits at the perimeter and what your developers install.

I track these with Intel60, a free tool I built that turns security news into 60-word, severity-rated briefs every 30 minutes: https://intel60.app

What was the biggest story on your radar this week? 👇

We published two more exploit and detection packages to @vulncheck's Initial Access community repo today. Neither CVE has been reported exploited in the wild yet, but both product ecosystems have been exploited in the past. Today's haul:

- CVE-2026-92122: A Groovy sandbox escape in the Jenkins Script Security Plugin, which (per Jenkins) is installed on 98.8% of all Jenkins controllers. FOFA shows ~80K Jenkins servers online.

- CVE-2026-8857: A command injection vulnerability in MediaWiki's EasyTimeline plugin (which was enabled in Fandom). A Nuclei template has been available since August, and there are ~25K MediaWiki instances online (which means that if this thing really hasn't been exploited yet - dubious - it probably will be).

Both #exploits come with Suricata and Snort rules, vulnerable target Docker containers, PCAPs, and ASM queries.

##
technotenshi@infosec.exchange at 2026-11-01T06:45:08.000Z ##

Linux kernel CVE-2026-98432 is a use-after-free in the IPv4 TCP handshake that a remote, unauthenticated peer can reach. Upstream is fixed, but Ubuntu's tracker still lists its kernels as vulnerable and Red Hat lists RHEL 8, 9 and 10 as affected. Anthropic says Claude built a root exploit for servers with a multipath TCP listener, which Go 1.24+ enables by default. The Go team says the bug is not specific to MPTCP.

https://atlasum.net/blog/cve-2026-98432-linux-ipv4-tcp-uaf

#LinuxKernel #InfoSec

dalias@hachyderm.io at 2026-11-01T18:49:42.000Z ##

@varjolintu Since you didn't cite the CVE IDs I can't check what you're talking about, but CVE-2026-118673 looks like bs. AFAICT it's "user can exploit themselves".

This is exactly what I mean by slop CVEs. Bugs that are *of the form* of a vuln (memory corruption, UAF, etc.) but where there is not any (or at least not any significant) crossing of privilege domains that make it attack surface.
